### 2. AppearancePreferencesManager - How Styles Are Applied
**File:** `/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/src/interface/home/AppearancePreferencesManager.ts`

**CSS Variables Generated (Lines 138-172):**

The manager exports three functions that return `CSSProperties` objects containing CSS variables:

**`getUiTextSizeStyle(level)` (Lines 138-154):**
Sets these CSS variables on the element:
```
--omni-ui-font-size
--omni-ui-xxs-size (10-14px based on level)
--omni-ui-xs-size (11-16px)
--omni-ui-sm-size (same as base uiSize)
--omni-ui-base-size (uiSize + 2)
--omni-ui-control-xs-size (uiSize + 12)
--omni-ui-control-sm-size (uiSize + 15)
--omni-ui-control-md-size (uiSize + 18)
--omni-ui-control-lg-size (uiSize + 24)
--omni-ui-icon-sm-size (uiSize + 1)
--omni-ui-icon-md-size (uiSize + 2)
```

Formula: `boostedPx(size, "--omni-mobile-ui-font-boost")` (Line 134-136)
- Allows runtime mobile boost adjustments via calc(): `calc(${size}px + var(${boostVariable}, 0px))`

**`getConversationTextSizeStyle(level)` (Lines 156-165):**
```
--omni-conversation-font-size
--omni-conversation-xs-size
--omni-conversation-sm-size
--omni-conversation-line-height
```

**`getTerminalTextSizeStyle(level)` (Lines 187-202):**
Scales these base pixel values by `terminalScale`:
```
--terminal-message-size (14px base)
--terminal-thought-size (13px base)
--terminal-thought-label-size (13px)
--terminal-tool-label-size (13px)
--terminal-tool-title-size (12px)
--terminal-pane-size (11px)
--terminal-pane-label-size (10px)
--terminal-badge-size (10px)
--terminal-permission-title-size (13px)
--terminal-permission-text-size (12px)
```

---

### 3. DOM Application - Where CSS Variables Are Applied
**File:** `/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/src/interface/home/HomeApp.tsx`

**Application Method (Lines 389-406):**
```jsx
// Lines 390-394: Get appearance preferences and generate styles
const appearancePreferences = useManagerSnapshot(appearancePreferencesManager);
const appearanceTextSizeStyle = useMemo(
  () => getAppearanceTextSizeStyle(appearancePreferences.uiTextSize, appearancePreferences.conversationTextSize),
  [appearancePreferences.conversationTextSize, appearancePreferences.uiTextSize],
);

// Lines 395-406: Apply to document.body
useEffect(() => {
  const body = document.body;
  const textSizeStyles = appearanceTextSizeStyle as Record<string, string | number | undefined>;
  body.classList.add("omni-app-text-scale");  // Add utility class
  for (const [property, value] of Object.entries(textSizeStyles)) {
    if (typeof value === "string" || typeof value === "number") 
      body.style.setProperty(property, String(value));  // Inline styles
  }
  return () => {
    for (const property of Object.keys(textSizeStyles)) 
      body.style.removeProperty(property);
    body.classList.remove("omni-app-text-scale");
  };
}, [appearanceTextSizeStyle]);
```

**Root Element (Lines 1034-1035, in return statement):**
```jsx
<div
  className="omni-app-text-scale flex h-dvh w-full overflow-hidden bg-background text-foreground lg:h-screen"
  style={appearanceTextSizeStyle}  // Style prop applied here too
>
```

**Method Used:** 
- **Inline styles via `style.setProperty()`** on `document.body`
- **CSS class `omni-app-text-scale`** added to body
- **Style prop** on root div element

---

### 4. Global CSS Variables and Consumption

```

**UI Text Size Scaling (Lines 350-407):**
The CSS uses the variables to scale Tailwind classes dynamically:

/* Control sizes (buttons, icons) - SCALE WITH VARIABLES */

}

.omni-app-text-scale .h-7.w-7,

}

}

/* Icon sizes inside controls */

/* Conversation text scaling */

.omni-app-text-scale .omni-conversation-text-scale .text-sm,

.omni-app-text-scale .omni-conversation-text-scale .leading-5,
.omni-app-text-scale .omni-conversation-text-scale .leading-6,

### 6. Tailwind Configuration and Rem-Based Scaling
**Package.json Dependencies:**
```json
"tailwindcss": "^4",
"@tailwindcss/postcss": "^4",
```

**PostCSS Config (/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/postcss.config.mjs):**
```javascript

  plugins: {

export default config;
```

**No explicit tailwind.config.js/ts found** — using PostCSS v4's inline configuration.

**Critical Finding About Rem Scaling:**
The system does **NOT rely on root font-size changes** for rem scaling. Instead:

1. The `omni-app-text-scale` class sets `font-size: var(--omni-ui-font-size)` on body (Line 337)
2. Tailwind's hardcoded classes (text-sm, text-xs, h-6, h-8, etc.) are **intercepted and replaced with CSS variables** via the `.omni-app-text-scale` utility overrides
3. Control sizes are **explicitly mapped to variable names** rather than relying on font-size inheritance

This is a **custom scaling system** that bypasses Tailwind's default rem-to-html-font-size chain. The appearance settings DO NOT change the root font-size, so rem-based elements outside the `omni-app-text-scale` scope would not scale.

AppearancePreferencesManager - How Styles Are Applied\n**File:** `/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/src/interface/home/AppearancePreferencesManager.ts`\n\n**CSS Variables Generated (Lines 138-172):**\n\nThe manager exports three functions that return `CSSProperties` objects containing CSS variables:\n\n**`getUiTextSizeStyle(level)` (Lines 138-154):**\nSets these CSS variables on the element:\n```\n--omni-ui-font-size\n--omni-ui-xxs-size (10-14px based on level)\n--omni-ui-xs-size (11-16px)\n--omni-ui-sm-size (same as base uiSize)\n--omni-ui-base-size (uiSize + 2)\n--omni-ui-control-xs-size (uiSize + 12)\n--omni-ui-control-sm-size (uiSize + 15)\n--omni-ui-control-md-size (uiSize + 18)\n--omni-ui-control-lg-size (uiSize + 24)\n--omni-ui-icon-sm-size (uiSize + 1)\n--omni-ui-icon-md-size (uiSize + 2)\n```\n\nFormula: `boostedPx(size, \"--omni-mobile-ui-font-boost\")` (Line 134-136)\n- Allows runtime mobile boost adjustments via calc(): `calc(${size}px + var(${boostVariable}, 0px))`\n\n**`getConversationTextSizeStyle(level)` (Lines 156-165):**\n```\n--omni-conversation-font-size\n--omni-conversation-xs-size\n--omni-conversation-sm-size\n--omni-conversation-line-height\n```\n\n**`getTerminalTextSizeStyle(level)` (Lines 187-202):**\nScales these base pixel values by `terminalScale`:\n```\n--terminal-message-size (14px base)\n--terminal-thought-size (13px base)\n--terminal-thought-label-size (13px)\n--terminal-tool-label-size (13px)\n--terminal-tool-title-size (12px)\n--terminal-pane-size (11px)\n--terminal-pane-label-size (10px)\n--terminal-badge-size (10px)\n--terminal-permission-title-size (13px)\n--terminal-permission-text-size (12px)\n```\n\n---\n\n### 3. DOM Application - Where CSS Variables Are Applied\n**File:** `/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/src/interface/home/HomeApp.tsx`\n\n**Application Method (Lines 389-406):**\n```jsx\n// Lines 390-394: Get appearance preferences and generate styles\nconst appearancePreferences = useManagerSnapshot(appearancePreferencesManager);\nconst appearanceTextSizeStyle = useMemo(\n  () => getAppearanceTextSizeStyle(appearancePreferences.uiTextSize, appearancePreferences.conversationTextSize),\n  [appearancePreferences.conversationTextSize, appearancePreferences.uiTextSize],\n);\n\n// Lines 395-406: Apply to document.body\nuseEffect(() => {\n  const body = document.body;\n  const textSizeStyles = appearanceTextSizeStyle as Record<string, string | number | undefined>;\n  body.classList.add(\"omni-app-text-scale\");  // Add utility class\n  for (const [property, value] of Object.entries(textSizeStyles)) {\n    if (typeof value === \"string\" || typeof value === \"number\") \n      body.style.setProperty(property, String(value));  // Inline styles\n  }\n  return () => {\n    for (const property of Object.keys(textSizeStyles)) \n      body.style.removeProperty(property);\n    body.classList.remove(\"omni-app-text-scale\");\n  };\n}, [appearanceTextSizeStyle]);\n```\n\n**Root Element (Lines 1034-1035, in return statement):**\n```jsx\n<div\n  className=\"omni-app-text-scale flex h-dvh w-full overflow-hidden bg-background text-foreground lg:h-screen\"\n  style={appearanceTextSizeStyle}  // Style prop applied here too\n>\n```\n\n**Method Used:** \n- **Inline styles via `style.setProperty()`** on `document.body`\n- **CSS class `omni-app-text-scale`** added to body\n- **Style prop** on root div element\n\n---\n\n### 4. Global CSS Variables and Consumption\n**File:** `/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/src/interface/styles/globals.css`\n\n**Mobile Boost Variables (Lines 330-348):**\n```css\n@layer utilities {\n  .omni-app-text-scale {\n    --omni-mobile-ui-font-boost: 0px;        /* 0px desktop, 1px mobile */\n    --omni-mobile-ui-control-boost: 0px;     /* 0px desktop, 2px mobile */\n    --omni-mobile-ui-icon-boost: 0px;        /* 0px desktop, 1px mobile */\n    --omni-mobile-conversation-font-boost: 0px;  /* 0px desktop, 1px mobile */\n    --omni-mobile-conversation-line-boost: 0px;  /* 0px desktop, 1px mobile */\n    font-size: var(--omni-ui-font-size);     /* Base font size set here */\n  }\n\n  @media (max-width: 640px) {\n    .omni-app-text-scale {\n      --omni-mobile-ui-font-boost: 1px;\n      --omni-mobile-ui-control-boost: 2px;\n      --omni-mobile-ui-icon-boost: 1px;\n      --omni-mobile-conversation-font-boost: 1px;\n      --omni-mobile-conversation-line-boost: 1px;\n    }\n  }\n```\n\n**UI Text Size Scaling (Lines 350-407):**\nThe CSS uses the variables to scale Tailwind classes dynamically:\n\n```css\n/* Text sizes */\n.omni-app-text-scale .text-[10px],\n.omni-app-text-scale .text-[11px] {\n  font-size: var(--omni-ui-xxs-size);\n}\n\n.omni-app-text-scale .text-xs,\n.omni-app-text-scale .text-[12px] {\n  font-size: var(--omni-ui-xs-size);\n}\n\n.omni-app-text-scale .text-sm,\n.omni-app-text-scale .text-[13px] {\n  font-size: var(--omni-ui-sm-size);\n}\n\n.omni-app-text-scale .text-base {\n  font-size: var(--omni-ui-base-size);\n}\n\n/* Control sizes (buttons, icons) - SCALE WITH VARIABLES */\n.omni-app-text-scale .h-6.w-6,\n.omni-app-text-scale .size-6 {\n  width: var(--omni-ui-control-xs-size);\n  height: var(--omni-ui-control-xs-size);\n}\n\n.omni-app-text-scale .h-7.w-7,\n.omni-app-text-scale .size-7 {\n  width: var(--omni-ui-control-sm-size);\n  height: var(--omni-ui-control-sm-size);\n}\n\n.omni-app-text-scale .h-8.w-8,\n.omni-app-text-scale .size-8 {\n  width: var(--omni-ui-control-md-size);\n  height: var(--omni-ui-control-md-size);\n}\n\n.omni-app-text-scale .h-9.w-9,\n.omni-app-text-scale .size-9 {\n  width: var(--omni-ui-control-lg-size);\n  height: var(--omni-ui-control-lg-size);\n}\n\n/* Icon sizes inside controls */\n.omni-app-text-scale .h-6.w-6 > svg,\n.omni-app-text-scale .size-6 > svg {\n  width: var(--omni-ui-icon-sm-size);\n  height: var(--omni-ui-icon-sm-size);\n}\n\n.omni-app-text-scale .h-8.w-8 > svg,\n.omni-app-text-scale .size-8 > svg {\n  width: var(--omni-ui-icon-md-size);\n  height: var(--omni-ui-icon-md-size);\n}\n\n/* Conversation text scaling */\n.omni-app-text-scale .omni-conversation-text-scale {\n  font-size: var(--omni-conversation-font-size);\n}\n\n.omni-app-text-scale .omni-conversation-text-scale .text-sm,\n.omni-app-text-scale .omni-conversation-text-scale .text-[13px] {\n  font-size: var(--omni-conversation-sm-size);\n}\n\n.omni-app-text-scale .omni-conversation-text-scale .leading-5,\n.omni-app-text-scale .omni-conversation-text-scale .leading-6,\n.omni-app-text-scale .omni-conversation-text-scale .leading-relaxed {\n  line-height: var(--omni-conversation-line-height);\n}\n```\n\n**Key Finding:** Control sizes (buttons, icon containers) are **NOT hardcoded in px/rem** — they scale dynamically via CSS variables.\n\n---\n\n### 5. Message Composer Input Font Size\n**File:** `/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/src/components/home/ConversationComposer.tsx`\n\n**Textarea Element (Line 265-392):**\n```jsx\n<textarea\n  data-composer-input=\"true\"\n  ref={commandInputRef}\n  // ... event handlers\n  className={cn(\n    \"omni-composer-input w-full resize-none bg-transparent text-[15px] outline-none\",\n    hasAttachments ? \"min-h-[152px] sm:min-h-[112px]\" : \"min-h-[112px] sm:min-h-[72px]\",\n    themeMode === \"night\"\n      ? \"text-foreground placeholder:text-muted-foreground/80\"\n      : \"text-[#454545] placeholder:text-[#c4c4c2] dark:text-foreground dark:placeholder:text-muted-foreground/80\",\n  )}\n/>\n```\n\n**Font Size:** **Hardcoded to `text-[15px]`** — NOT tied to appearance settings variables.\n\n**CSS Class Definition (globals.css, Lines 435-443):**\n```css\n.omni-composer-input {\n  line-height: 20px;\n}\n\n@media (min-width: 640px) {\n  .omni-composer-input {\n    line-height: 24px;\n  }\n}\n```\n\n**Finding:** The composer textarea uses a **fixed 15px font size** and is **independent of the UI font size setting**. Only line-height is responsive.\n\n---\n\n### 6. Tailwind Configuration and Rem-Based Scaling\n**Package.json Dependencies:**\n```json\n\"tailwindcss\": \"^4\",\n\"@tailwindcss/postcss\": \"^4\",\n```\n\n**PostCSS Config (/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/postcss.config.mjs):**\n```javascript\nconst config = {\n  plugins: {\n    \"@tailwindcss/postcss\": {},\n  },\n};\nexport default config;\n```\n\n**No explicit tailwind.config.js/ts found** — using PostCSS v4's inline configuration.\n\n**Critical Finding About Rem Scaling:**\nThe system does **NOT rely on root font-size changes** for rem scaling. Instead:\n\n1. The `omni-app-text-scale` class sets `font-size: var(--omni-ui-font-size)` on body (Line 337)\n2. Tailwind's hardcoded classes (text-sm, text-xs, h-6, h-8, etc.) are **intercepted and replaced with CSS variables** via the `.omni-app-text-scale` utility overrides\n3. Control sizes are **explicitly mapped to variable names** rather than relying on font-size inheritance\n\nThis is a **custom scaling system** that bypasses Tailwind's default rem-to-html-font-size chain. The appearance settings DO NOT change the root font-size, so rem-based elements outside the `omni-app-text-scale` scope would not scale.\n\n---\n\n## Summary Table\n\n| Aspect | Details |\n|--------|---------|\n| **Settings Panel** | `/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/src/components/settings/AppearanceSettingsPanel.tsx` |\n| **Font Sizes Available** | UI, Conversation, Terminal (5 levels each: tiny→huge) |\n| **Storage** | localStorage with keys: `omni-ui-font-size`, `omni-conversation-font-size`, `omni-terminal-text-size` |\n| **CSS Variables Generated** | 11 UI variables + 4 conversation + 10 terminal = 25 total |\n| **Application Method** | Inline styles via `document.body.style.setProperty()` + `omni-app-text-scale` class |\n| **Control Scaling** | Button heights (h-6/7/8/9), icon sizes scale via variables |\n| **Composer Input Font** | **Hardcoded 15px** — independent of settings |\n| **Line Height** | Conversation: variable via `--omni-conversation-line-height`; Composer: fixed 20/24px |\n| **Mobile Boosting** | Yes — mobile viewport adds +1–2px boost via `--omni-mobile-*-boost` variables |\n| **Tailwind Usage** | v4 with PostCSS; no rem scaling via root font-size (custom variable system instead) |\n| **Scaling Scope** | Limited to elements with `.omni-app-text-scale` ancestor + specific Tailwind class combinations |"
  }

50	  terminalTextSize: "default",
51	  alwaysExpandThoughts: false,
52	  savedUiTextSize: "default",
53	  savedConversationTextSize: "default",
54	  savedTerminalTextSize: "default",
55	  savedAlwaysExpandThoughts: false,
56	  dirtyKeys: new Set(),
57	  hydrated: false,
58	};
59	
60	function canUseLocalStorage() {
61	  return typeof window !== "undefined" && typeof window.localStorage !== "undefined";
62	}
63	
64	function parseTextSize(value: string | null): AppearanceTextSizeLevel {
65	  if (TEXT_SIZE_LEVELS.some((level) => level.value === value)) {
66	    return value as AppearanceTextSizeLevel;
67	  }
68	
69	  switch (value) {
70	    case "compact":
71	      return "small";
72	    case "larger":
73	    case "largest":
74	      return "huge";
75	    default:
76	      return "default";
77	  }
78	}
79	
80	function writeLocalPreference(key: string, value: string) {
81	  if (!canUseLocalStorage()) {
82	    return;
83	  }
84	
85	  window.localStorage.setItem(key, value);
86	}
87	
88	function removeLocalPreference(key: string) {
89	  if (!canUseLocalStorage()) {
90	    return;
91	  }
92	
93	  window.localStorage.removeItem(key);
94	}
95	
96	function draftValues(state: AppearancePreferencesState): AppearanceDraftValues {
97	  return {
98	    uiTextSize: state.uiTextSize,
99	    conversationTextSize: state.conversationTextSize,
100	    terminalTextSize: state.terminalTextSize,
101	    alwaysExpandThoughts: state.alwaysExpandThoughts,
102	  };
103	}
104	
105	function savedValues(state: AppearancePreferencesState): AppearanceDraftValues {
106	  return {
107	    uiTextSize: state.savedUiTextSize,
108	    conversationTextSize: state.savedConversationTextSize,
109	    terminalTextSize: state.savedTerminalTextSize,
110	    alwaysExpandThoughts: state.savedAlwaysExpandThoughts,
111	  };
112	}
113	
114	function getAppearanceDirtyKeys(draft: AppearanceDraftValues, saved: AppearanceDraftValues) {
115	  const dirtyKeys = new Set<AppearancePreferenceKey>();
116	
117	  for (const key of APPEARANCE_PREFERENCE_KEYS) {
118	    if (draft[key] !== saved[key]) {
119	      dirtyKeys.add(key);
120	    }
121	  }
122	
123	  return dirtyKeys;
124	}
125	
126	function toScaledPx(baseSize: number, scale: number) {
127	  return `${Math.round(baseSize * scale * 10) / 10}px`;
128	}
129	
130	function textSizeLevel(level: AppearanceTextSizeLevel) {
131	  return TEXT_SIZE_LEVELS.find((candidate) => candidate.value === level) ?? TEXT_SIZE_LEVELS[2];
132	}
133	
134	function boostedPx(size: number, boostVariable: string) {
135	  return `calc(${size}px + var(${boostVariable}, 0px))`;
136	}
137	
138	export function getUiTextSizeStyle(level: UiTextSizeLevel): CSSProperties {
139	  const textSize = textSizeLevel(level);
140	
141	  return {
142	    "--omni-ui-font-size": boostedPx(textSize.uiSize, "--omni-mobile-ui-font-boost"),
143	    "--omni-ui-xxs-size": boostedPx(Math.max(10, textSize.uiSize - 4), "--omni-mobile-ui-font-boost"),
144	    "--omni-ui-xs-size": boostedPx(Math.max(11, textSize.uiSize - 2), "--omni-mobile-ui-font-boost"),
145	    "--omni-ui-sm-size": boostedPx(textSize.uiSize, "--omni-mobile-ui-font-boost"),
146	    "--omni-ui-base-size": boostedPx(textSize.uiSize + 2, "--omni-mobile-ui-font-boost"),
147	    "--omni-ui-control-xs-size": boostedPx(textSize.uiSize + 12, "--omni-mobile-ui-control-boost"),
148	    "--omni-ui-control-sm-size": boostedPx(textSize.uiSize + 15, "--omni-mobile-ui-control-boost"),
149	    "--omni-ui-control-md-size": boostedPx(textSize.uiSize + 18, "--omni-mobile-ui-control-boost"),
150	    "--omni-ui-control-lg-size": boostedPx(textSize.uiSize + 24, "--omni-mobile-ui-control-boost"),
151	    "--omni-ui-icon-sm-size": boostedPx(textSize.uiSize + 1, "--omni-mobile-ui-icon-boost"),
152	    "--omni-ui-icon-md-size": boostedPx(textSize.uiSize + 2, "--omni-mobile-ui-icon-boost"),
153	  } as CSSProperties;
154	}
155	
156	export function getConversationTextSizeStyle(level: ConversationTextSizeLevel): CSSProperties {
157	  const textSize = textSizeLevel(level);
158	
159	  return {
160	    "--omni-conversation-font-size": boostedPx(textSize.conversationSize, "--omni-mobile-conversation-font-boost"),
161	    "--omni-conversation-xs-size": boostedPx(Math.max(12, textSize.conversationSize - 2), "--omni-mobile-conversation-font-boost"),
162	    "--omni-conversation-sm-size": boostedPx(textSize.conversationSize, "--omni-mobile-conversation-font-boost"),
163	    "--omni-conversation-line-height": boostedPx(textSize.conversationLineHeight, "--omni-mobile-conversation-line-boost"),
164	  } as CSSProperties;
165	}
166	
167	export function getAppearanceTextSizeStyle(uiTextSize: UiTextSizeLevel, conversationTextSize: ConversationTextSizeLevel): CSSProperties {
168	  return {
169	    ...getUiTextSizeStyle(uiTextSize),
170	    ...getConversationTextSizeStyle(conversationTextSize),
171	  } as CSSProperties;
172	}
173	
174	const TERMINAL_BASE_FONT_SIZES = {
175	  message: 14,
176	  thought: 13,
177	  thoughtLabel: 13,
178	  toolLabel: 13,
179	  toolTitle: 12,
180	  pane: 11,
181	  paneLabel: 10,
182	  badge: 10,
183	  permissionTitle: 13,
184	  permissionText: 12,
185	};
186	
187	export function getTerminalTextSizeStyle(level: TerminalTextSizeLevel): CSSProperties {
188	  const scale = textSizeLevel(level).terminalScale;
189	
190	  return {
191	    "--terminal-message-size": toScaledPx(TERMINAL_BASE_FONT_SIZES.message, scale),
192	    "--terminal-thought-size": toScaledPx(TERMINAL_BASE_FONT_SIZES.thought, scale),
193	    "--terminal-thought-label-size": toScaledPx(TERMINAL_BASE_FONT_SIZES.thoughtLabel, scale),
194	    "--terminal-tool-label-size": toScaledPx(TERMINAL_BASE_FONT_SIZES.toolLabel, scale),
195	    "--terminal-tool-title-size": toScaledPx(TERMINAL_BASE_FONT_SIZES.toolTitle, scale),
196	    "--terminal-pane-size": toScaledPx(TERMINAL_BASE_FONT_SIZES.pane, scale),
197	    "--terminal-pane-label-size": toScaledPx(TERMINAL_BASE_FONT_SIZES.paneLabel, scale),
198	    "--terminal-badge-size": toScaledPx(TERMINAL_BASE_FONT_SIZES.badge, scale),
199	    "--terminal-permission-title-size": toScaledPx(TERMINAL_BASE_FONT_SIZES.permissionTitle, scale),
200	    "--terminal-permission-text-size": toScaledPx(TERMINAL_BASE_FONT_SIZES.permissionText, scale),
201	  } as CSSProperties;
202	}
203	
204	export function getConversationTerminalTextSizeStyle(level: ConversationTextSizeLevel): CSSProperties {
205	  return getTerminalTextSizeStyle(level);
206	}
207	
208	export class AppearancePreferencesManager extends StateManager<AppearancePreferencesState> {
209	  constructor() {
210	    super(DEFAULT_APPEARANCE_PREFERENCES);
211	  }
212	
213	  hydrateFromLocalStorage() {
214	    if (!canUseLocalStorage()) {
215	      this.setKey("hydrated", true);
216	      return;
217	    }
218	
219	    const uiTextSize = parseTextSize(window.localStorage.getItem(UI_TEXT_SIZE_STORAGE_KEY));
220	    const conversationTextSize = parseTextSize(
221	      window.localStorage.getItem(CONVERSATION_TEXT_SIZE_STORAGE_KEY)
222	        ?? window.localStorage.getItem(LEGACY_DIRECT_TEXT_SIZE_STORAGE_KEY),
223	    );
224	    const terminalTextSize = parseTextSize(window.localStorage.getItem(TERMINAL_TEXT_SIZE_STORAGE_KEY));
225	    const alwaysExpandThoughts = window.localStorage.getItem(ALWAYS_EXPAND_THOUGHTS_STORAGE_KEY) === "true";
226	
227	    this.patch({
228	      uiTextSize,
229	      conversationTextSize,
230	      terminalTextSize,
231	      alwaysExpandThoughts,
232	      savedUiTextSize: uiTextSize,
233	      savedConversationTextSize: conversationTextSize,
234	      savedTerminalTextSize: terminalTextSize,
235	      savedAlwaysExpandThoughts: alwaysExpandThoughts,
236	      dirtyKeys: new Set(),
237	      hydrated: true,
238	    });
239	  }
240	
241	  private setDraft(values: Partial<AppearanceDraftValues>) {
242	    this.patch((current) => {
243	      const draft = { ...draftValues(current), ...values };
244	
245	      return {
246	        ...draft,
247	        dirtyKeys: getAppearanceDirtyKeys(draft, savedValues(current)),
248	        hydrated: true,
249	      };
250	    });
251	  }
252	
253	  setUiTextSize(uiTextSize: UiTextSizeLevel) {
254	    this.setDraft({ uiTextSize });
255	  }
256	
257	  setConversationTextSize(conversationTextSize: ConversationTextSizeLevel) {
258	    this.setDraft({ conversationTextSize });
259	  }
260	
261	  setTerminalTextSize(terminalTextSize: TerminalTextSizeLevel) {
262	    this.setDraft({ terminalTextSize });
263	  }
264	
265	  setAlwaysExpandThoughts(alwaysExpandThoughts: boolean) {
266	    this.setDraft({ alwaysExpandThoughts });
267	  }
268	
269	  saveDraft() {
270	    const { uiTextSize, conversationTextSize, terminalTextSize, alwaysExpandThoughts } = this.getSnapshot();
271	    writeLocalPreference(UI_TEXT_SIZE_STORAGE_KEY, uiTextSize);
272	    writeLocalPreference(CONVERSATION_TEXT_SIZE_STORAGE_KEY, conversationTextSize);
273	    writeLocalPreference(TERMINAL_TEXT_SIZE_STORAGE_KEY, terminalTextSize);
274	    writeLocalPreference(ALWAYS_EXPAND_THOUGHTS_STORAGE_KEY, alwaysExpandThoughts ? "true" : "false");
275	    this.patch({
276	      savedUiTextSize: uiTextSize,
277	      savedConversationTextSize: conversationTextSize,
278	      savedTerminalTextSize: terminalTextSize,
279	      savedAlwaysExpandThoughts: alwaysExpandThoughts,
280	      dirtyKeys: new Set(),
281	      hydrated: true,
282	    });
283	  }
284	
285	  discardDraft() {
286	    this.patch((current) => ({
287	      ...savedValues(current),
288	      dirtyKeys: new Set(),
289	      hydrated: true,
290	    }));
291	  }
292	
293	  reset() {
294	    removeLocalPreference(UI_TEXT_SIZE_STORAGE_KEY);
295	    removeLocalPreference(CONVERSATION_TEXT_SIZE_STORAGE_KEY);
296	    removeLocalPreference(TERMINAL_TEXT_SIZE_STORAGE_KEY);
297	    removeLocalPreference(ALWAYS_EXPAND_THOUGHTS_STORAGE_KEY);
298	    removeLocalPreference(LEGACY_DIRECT_TEXT_SIZE_STORAGE_KEY);
299	    this.patch({
300	      ...DEFAULT_APPEARANCE_PREFERENCES,
301	      dirtyKeys: new Set(),
302	    });
303	  }
304	}
305	
306	export const appearancePreferencesManager = new AppearancePreferencesManager();
307

## Claude thinking · 2026-08-03T17:42:26.938Z

I'm seeing that the current scaling only affects square icon buttons with the `.h-N.w-N` pattern, while non-square buttons with padding and gaps aren't covered. The composer textarea also uses a custom font size that's not in the scaling list. I need to figure out the best way to make all UI elements scale consistently—whether that means expanding the override list or taking a different approach entirely.

The cleaner solution would be to leverage Tailwind v4's spacing variable system. Since Tailwind v4 uses `calc(var(--spacing) * N)` for all spacing utilities, I could override the `--spacing` variable on the scale container to proportionally scale everything—heights, widths, padding, gaps, margins—all at once. That's much more elegant than whack-a-mole CSS overrides. I need to verify that this project's globals.css actually uses that pattern and check whether `--spacing` is already being set anywhere.

The tradeoff is that scaling all spacing uniformly could break layouts—a sidebar at `w-64` would grow 15% at "large" scale, which might be problematic. I should look at the actual theme definitions in globals.css and examine the composer and settings panel to see if there's already a mobile UI scale concept in place.

I'm checking whether Tailwind v4 uses CSS custom properties for spacing calculations—specifically if utilities like `h-8` compile to `height: calc(var(--spacing) * 8)` with a default `--spacing: 0.25rem`. I can verify this by looking at the Tailwind package directly.

The key insight is that if I set `--spacing` on the `.omni-app-text-scale` element at runtime, all descendant spacing utilities would automatically recompute since they reference that variable. This would scale padding, gaps, widths, and heights uniformly—which is what we want for "buttons should just grow." The tradeoff is that fixed dimensions like the `w-64` sidebar would also scale, which might actually be desirable when text is bigger. Arbitrary rem values in things like `max-w-[26rem]` won't scale though, since they're hardcoded.

With `--spacing` scaled and the existing text-size rules in place, the design stays consistent. But I'm weighing whether scaling all spacing uniformly is the right call, or if a more restrained approach—scaling `--spacing` modestly (like 0.9 to 1.25) while keeping explicit control over specific elements—would be safer.

The real issue is that the current ad-hoc control-size rules (like `.h-8.w-8` with explicit `--omni-ui-control-md-size` variables) would conflict with `--spacing`-based scaling. At "huge" text, the control variable might be 36px while a scaled `h-8` becomes 41px—inconsistent. The cleaner solution is to ditch those hand-maintained rules entirely and let `--spacing` drive everything uniformly, including the SVG sizing inside buttons. That removes brittleness and makes the system much more maintainable.

Now I need to check whether anything relies on `--spacing` being exactly 0.25rem for layout that shouldn't scale—like xterm terminal sizing. Scaling inside the terminal panel should be fine. Also verifying that setting `--spacing` on the body and root div covers portals too, since they render into document.body and inherit the class and inline variables.

For the composer input font size issue, the user's text isn't scaling with the font size settings. I should tie the composer input to the conversation font size since the text you type becomes a conversation message, not a UI element. I'll add a floor so it never goes below 15px.

Actually, wait — the current hardcoded 15px is already larger than the default conversation size of 14px, and on iOS Safari that triggers an automatic zoom-on-focus when the font is under 16px. Since the user is on mobile, I could use `max(16px, conversation-size)` to prevent that zoom behavior, which would be a nice improvement. Let me check if there's a viewport meta tag with `user-scalable=no` that might already be preventing this.

For the rename modal on mobile, I'm planning to add a Dialog-based rename modal that only appears on smaller screens (below lg, matching the existing pattern for the mobile title button). I'll also hide the folder label and workspace badge from the header on mobile to clean up the interface.

The user's complaint about seeing "omniharness" repeated makes sense now — the title area shows the session title, then the folder label "omniharness", and the workspace badge also displays "omniharness" as the last path segment. That's the duplication! The workspace badge falls back to showing the last path segment when it's not a worktree setup.

The fix is to avoid rendering the root folder label when it would duplicate what the workspace badge already shows, and on mobile hide both labels entirely to keep just the title. Let me check the RunWorkspaceBadge implementation to confirm how it determines what to display.

## Claude thinking · 2026-08-03T17:46:52.485Z

I'm starting the implementation by adding a spacing scale to the appearance preferences manager. I'll define the UI scale levels with specific multipliers and then update the text size style function to include a spacing variable calculated from the scale, though I'm realizing that applying `--spacing` directly to the body might cause it to inherit everywhere.

Setting the spacing variable on the body will override Tailwind's default theme spacing for all descendants, which means spacing utilities like padding, margin, width, and height will all scale proportionally — the sidebar and header will grow along with the text. This is actually the intended behavior. Since the spacing variable already handles icon scaling through utilities like `h-4 w-4`, I can remove the explicit icon size overrides that were previously needed.

However, those old control size rules use font-size-derived variables that would create inconsistencies with the new spacing-based approach — at different text scales, icon buttons would end up slightly different sizes than neighboring buttons. It's cleaner to drop those rules entirely and let the spacing variable handle sizing uniformly. This means the mobile control boost variables become unused, so I should check if any tests are asserting on them before removing them.

For the composer textarea, I'm replacing the hardcoded font size with a CSS variable approach that derives from the conversation text size plus one step, preserving the current 15px default while respecting the mobile boost. The font sizes would scale as tiny 12→13, small 13→14, default 14→15, large 16→17, huge 18→19. Since the current 15px is already below the iOS zoom threshold, there's no regression to worry about.

I'm also considering whether to add a media query with `max()` to force 16px on mobile and prevent zoom-on-focus, but that would override the user's tiny setting, so I'll skip it for now and just note it as an option. I should check if the app's viewport meta already has `maximum-scale=1` set, which would disable zoom entirely. I'll also tie the line-height to the conversation line-height variable for consistency.

For the mobile title editing, I'm thinking about replacing the inline input with a Dialog on small screens. The tricky part is that `isEditingTitle` currently controls the inline input for both mobile and desktop, so I need to keep it inline on larger screens but show a Dialog modal on mobile. I could render both conditionally—hiding the inline input on mobile and showing the Dialog instead—but then I'd have two inputs bound to the same state, and the focus logic needs to target the right one depending on the screen size.

The issue is that `.focus()` on a hidden element doesn't work, so I need a cleaner approach. I'm considering using a media query hook that matches the `lg` breakpoint (1024px) instead of relying on `useIsMobile()` at 768px, since the header's title button already uses `lg:` for visibility. That way the Dialog becomes the editing surface for anything below `lg`, keeping the breakpoints consistent.

I'll check if there's already a generic media query hook in the codebase or add `useIsCompactLayout()` to the existing hooks file. Then in the header component, I can conditionally render either a Dialog with input and buttons for compact layouts, or the inline input for desktop. I'll also need to add i18n strings for the dialog labels, which means updating the locale files with new keys like `session.rename.title` and related fields.

Now I'm checking the locale files to see if `common.save` and `common.cancel` already exist so I can reuse them. For the mobile layout, I'll hide the folder label and badge on smaller screens using responsive classes, then deduplicate the badge label logic to avoid showing the same text twice.

The cleanest approach is to export a helper function from RunWorkspaceBadge that resolves the workspace label, then use that same function in HomeHeader to decide when to hide the folder label. Let me run the gathering commands to see what we're working with.

1	import { StateManager, type StateListener } from "@/lib/state-manager"
2	import { useManagerSnapshot } from "@/lib/use-manager-snapshot"
3	
4	const MOBILE_BREAKPOINT = 768
5	
6	class ViewportManager extends StateManager<{ isMobile: boolean }> {
7	  private mediaQuery: MediaQueryList | null = null
8	  private listenerCount = 0
9	
10	  constructor() {
11	    super({ isMobile: false })
12	  }
13	
14	  private readonly handleChange = () => {
15	    this.setKey("isMobile", window.innerWidth < MOBILE_BREAKPOINT)
16	  }
17	
18	  override subscribe(listener: StateListener) {
19	    if (typeof window !== "undefined" && this.listenerCount === 0) {
20	      this.mediaQuery = window.matchMedia(`(max-width: ${MOBILE_BREAKPOINT - 1}px)`)
21	      this.mediaQuery.addEventListener("change", this.handleChange)
22	      this.handleChange()
23	    }
24	    this.listenerCount += 1
25	    const unsubscribe = super.subscribe(listener)
26	    return () => {
27	      unsubscribe()
28	      this.listenerCount -= 1
29	      if (this.listenerCount === 0) {
30	        this.mediaQuery?.removeEventListener("change", this.handleChange)
31	        this.mediaQuery = null
32	      }
33	    }
34	  }
35	}
36	
37	export const viewportManager = new ViewportManager()
38	
39	export function useIsMobile() {
40	  return useManagerSnapshot(viewportManager).isMobile
41	}
42

## Claude thinking · 2026-08-03T17:47:29.852Z

The tests are expecting specific control size values, so I can't just remove those rules. I need to redefine the control size variables to align with the spacing scale instead of being based on font size. The current test expects `--omni-ui-control-xs-size` to be `calc(26px + var(--omni-mobile-ui-control-boost, 0px))`, where 26px comes from 14 plus 12, but I need to figure out how this maps to the scaled spacing system.

My approach is to keep the control variables but derive them from the base spacing values—so `--omni-ui-control-xs-size` becomes something like `calc(24px * scale + boost)` where 24px is the natural h-6 size. This means at default scale the icon buttons would shrink slightly (26px → 24px for xs, 38px → 36px for lg), which is more consistent but does change the visual appearance. I'll need to update the test assertions to match the new values. and let spacing handle all the sizing. I'd update the tests to verify this new approach works, which means removing about forty lines of redundant CSS rules for controls and icons. Icons inside buttons will scale with the UI font size through spacing, which is equivalent to the current behavior. There's a small risk that arbitrary values like `h-[18px]` won't scale, but that's acceptable since most icon sizing uses standard Tailwind classes. I'm checking whether the spacing variable actually applies when set inline on the body element. I need to verify how Tailwind actually emits the spacing scale in this setup—whether the default `--spacing` variable remains a runtime reference or gets inlined. Rather than guessing, I should test this empirically by building the CSS and checking the output directly, possibly using the Tailwind CLI or PostCSS programmatically. I'll run the compile check to verify the spacing utilities are generated correctly, and then update the test assertions in the terminal-fit test file that reads from globals.css.

## Claude thinking · 2026-08-03T17:48:29.404Z

I'm verifying that the spacing utilities use `calc()` with a CSS variable multiplier, and that setting `--spacing` on the body element will cascade down to scale all spacing throughout that subtree. The regex pattern matches shadcn's variant structure, and I'm confirming that `--spacing` is defined at the root level so any override lower in the DOM takes precedence.

Now I'm ready to implement the solution. I'll add a `uiScale` property to the text size levels (ranging from 0.92 for tiny up to 1.3 for huge), then modify the style generation to set `--spacing` as a calculated value that multiplies the base 0.25rem by the chosen scale factor. The mobile font boost that already exists will stay in place, but since the user selected a global preference rather than a mobile-specific option, there's no additional boost needed for the spacing calculation itself.

For the control buttons and icons, I'm considering whether to keep the explicit size rules or delete them since they'd become redundant once everything scales uniformly through the `--spacing` variable. The cleaner approach is to remove those rules and let the scaling happen through a single lever, which means updating the tests accordingly. Icon buttons will still scale properly when the user adjusts the UI font size because that font size level maps directly to the scale factor. The mobile boost variables become unused once I remove those rules, so I'll clean those out too. For globals.css, I'm keeping the font boosts in `.omni-app-text-scale` but removing the control and icon sizing rules entirely. Then I'm adding specific font-size and line-height rules for the composer input that pull from CSS variables, with a media query for larger screens.

Actually, I'm reconsidering the line-height approach — instead of hardcoding mobile and desktop values, I could just use the conversation line-height variable everywhere, which defaults to 24px. That's slightly looser on mobile than the original 20px, but it's more readable and the textarea's min-height classes will handle it fine.

For the font-size responsiveness issue, I need to apply the composer variables directly to the input element with a fallback. I'll set both font-size and line-height using CSS variables that come from the text scale style, and remove the hardcoded Tailwind class. The root div always gets the scale class and style prop, so the variables should be available — but I need a fallback in case SSR renders before the effect runs.

For HomeHeader, I'm adding a compact layout hook to detect screens below lg breakpoint, then conditioning the edit mode between a dialog on mobile and inline input on desktop. The folder label and workspace badge need to hide on mobile to save space, and the RunWorkspaceBadge should also be hidden below lg.

For deduplication, I'll export a helper from RunWorkspaceBadge that extracts the branch label, then use that in the header to show the folder label only when there's a branch label present — otherwise the badge already displays the folder name, making the label redundant. Since both components use the same fallback logic for the path, they'll stay in sync.

Now I need to add i18n keys for the rename dialog across all locale files — "Rename session" for the title and "Session name" for the label, reusing the existing cancel and save keys. I'll add these to all 10 locale files and check if there's a test that validates locale key parity.

  t,

      { value: "ja", label: "Japanese" },
      { value: "ko", label: "Korean" },
    ]);
  });

    const snapshot = i18nManager.getSnapshot();

    expect(i18nManager.getSnapshot()).toBe(snapshot);
  });

  it("loads non-English locale resources on demand", async () => {
    expect(i18nManager.getSnapshot().loadedLocales).toEqual(["en"]);

    await i18nManager.setLocaleAsync("es");

    expect(Object.keys(localeLoaders)).toEqual(["es", "fr", "de", "it", "pt", "zh-CN", "ja", "ko"]);
  });

    const englishKeys = Object.keys(en).sort();

## Claude thinking · 2026-08-03T17:48:56.678Z

I'm refactoring the style function to use `--spacing` and `--omni-ui-scale` instead of the control and icon variables, where `--spacing` gets calculated as `0.25rem` multiplied by the scale factor, and keeping `--omni-ui-scale` exported for cases that need manual scaling adjustments. The `--spacing` variable will scale header heights and other layout dimensions, which is good. I need to check whether any components hardcode pixel sizes in JavaScript—like the terminal fit logic or virtualized list row heights—that might break when spacing scales. Let me search the codebase and make the necessary changes.

  "old_string": "const MOBILE_BREAKPOINT = 768\n\nclass ViewportManager extends StateManager<{ isMobile: boolean }> {\n  private mediaQuery: MediaQueryList | null = null\n  private listenerCount = 0\n\n  constructor() {\n    super({ isMobile: false })\n  }\n\n  private readonly handleChange = () => {\n    this.setKey(\"isMobile\", window.innerWidth < MOBILE_BREAKPOINT)\n  }\n\n  override subscribe(listener: StateListener) {\n    if (typeof window !== \"undefined\" && this.listenerCount === 0) {\n      this.mediaQuery = window.matchMedia(`(max-width: ${MOBILE_BREAKPOINT - 1}px)`)\n      this.mediaQuery.addEventListener(\"change\", this.handleChange)\n      this.handleChange()\n    }\n    this.listenerCount += 1\n    const unsubscribe = super.subscribe(listener)\n    return () => {\n      unsubscribe()\n      this.listenerCount -= 1\n      if (this.listenerCount === 0) {\n        this.mediaQuery?.removeEventListener(\"change\", this.handleChange)\n        this.mediaQuery = null\n      }\n    }\n  }\n}\n\nexport const viewportManager = new ViewportManager()\n\nexport function useIsMobile() {\n  return useManagerSnapshot(viewportManager).isMobile\n}",
  "new_string": "const MOBILE_BREAKPOINT = 768\nconst COMPACT_LAYOUT_BREAKPOINT = 1024\n\nclass ViewportManager extends StateManager<{ isBelowBreakpoint: boolean }> {\n  private mediaQuery: MediaQueryList | null = null\n  private listenerCount = 0\n\n  constructor(private readonly breakpoint: number) {\n    super({ isBelowBreakpoint: false })\n  }\n\n  private readonly handleChange = () => {\n    this.setKey(\"isBelowBreakpoint\", window.innerWidth < this.breakpoint)\n  }\n\n  override subscribe(listener: StateListener) {\n    if (typeof window !== \"undefined\" && this.listenerCount === 0) {\n      this.mediaQuery = window.matchMedia(`(max-width: ${this.breakpoint - 1}px)`)\n      this.mediaQuery.addEventListener(\"change\", this.handleChange)\n      this.handleChange()\n    }\n    this.listenerCount += 1\n    const unsubscribe = super.subscribe(listener)\n    return () => {\n      unsubscribe()\n      this.listenerCount -= 1\n      if (this.listenerCount === 0) {\n        this.mediaQuery?.removeEventListener(\"change\", this.handleChange)\n        this.mediaQuery = null\n      }\n    }\n  }\n}\n\nexport const viewportManager = new ViewportManager(MOBILE_BREAKPOINT)\n\n/** Matches the `lg:` breakpoint the app uses to switch to its phone/tablet chrome. */\nexport const compactLayoutManager = new ViewportManager(COMPACT_LAYOUT_BREAKPOINT)\n\nexport function useIsMobile() {\n  return useManagerSnapshot(viewportManager).isBelowBreakpoint\n}\n\nexport function useIsCompactLayout() {\n  return useManagerSnapshot(compactLayoutManager).isBelowBreakpoint\n}"
}

## Claude thinking · 2026-08-03T17:50:21.596Z

I'm noticing a logic issue with the title label gate — when there's no selected run, `titleLabel` is undefined, so it falls back to `rootFolderLabel`, but that also requires a run with a branch to exist. This means in a new conversation state where no run is selected, the header won't display the folder label like it used to, even though `RunWorkspaceBadge` already handles the null case properly.

Actually, that's probably fine since the user complained about duplication and the folder is already shown in the input controls via `BranchWorkspaceButton` when there's no selected run — so removing it from the header aligns with their feedback. I should keep `rootFolderLabel` showing only when there's both a run and a branch label, which keeps the behavior predictable.

For the focus management, I need to update the ref to only focus when using the inline input mode, and add a separate ref for the dialog input. Then I'll update the JSX accordingly.

parent: 29d56d75-d9f5-4913-a790-5b8d9905a569

}

## Claude thinking · 2026-08-03T17:53:00.309Z

I need to add tests for the mobile rename dialog and hidden folder/branch handling in the conversation-actions test file, using source assertions to verify that the HomeHeader component includes the compact layout logic.

I'm also checking whether the conversation-actions test validates i18n keys, and thinking through the edge case where the dialog closes when the layout switches from compact to full-width during a resize—since the `open` prop is controlled, React will just close it without necessarily firing `onOpenChange`.

There's a potential focus management issue with the dialog input: when the dialog opens, Base UI's focus trapping might override the ref-focus I'm setting on the input. Adding an `autoFocus` attribute to the dialog's input field should ensure it gets focused properly before the dialog's own focus logic takes over.

## Claude thinking · 2026-08-03T17:53:12.255Z

I'm working through the focus behavior with Base UI's Dialog — the Input is the first tabbable element so it gets focused automatically on open, but our callback ref tries to select the text at mount before Base UI's focus effect runs, then Base UI focuses it again which doesn't preserve the selection. The timing is causing the selection to be lost.

I could pass `initialFocus` to DialogContent with a ref to make it deterministic, but keeping it simple with the default behavior is acceptable even if the text isn't selected. For the mobile tap target on the title button, I'm adding vertical padding to make it taller — something like `py-1.5` should give around a 34px tap target. Since the parent uses `items-baseline` alignment, the extra padding will shift the button vertically but keep the baselines aligned, so it should be fine. Now I'll make that edit, add the tests, and run the full suite.

uuid: a998e742-1ee8-44e1-99ad-00eb81edbcfe
parent: 9208c2a7-3733-46d1-9131-0d97f777e529

I'm reconsidering how `rootFolderLabel` behaves now—it only displays when there's a branch label present. On desktop for a regular session, the header will show the title and folder badge, whereas before it showed title, folder name, and badge. That's acceptable. But for a new conversation with no selected run, the folder name area is now empty instead of showing the folder name like it used to, and I'm wondering if that's the right call.

Actually, looking at it more carefully: when both `titleLabel` and `rootFolderLabel` are empty, the whole block renders as null, so the top bar loses the working directory hint on new conversations. But the composer's BranchWorkspaceButton handles this—it shows up specifically when there's no selected run ID, so users still get that context. The behavior aligns with what was described. Let me verify the HomeHeader diff renders correctly and check the git stats to confirm I haven't modified anything unintended.

## Claude thinking · 2026-08-03T17:56:00.342Z

The sidebar width is a JavaScript pixel value that's user-resizable, so it won't scale with Tailwind utilities—which is actually what we want. The panel widths stay fixed once the user sets them, and only the spacing within components responds to scaling. That's a better outcome than I initially worried about.

## Claude · 2026-08-03T17:56:14.715Z

All four items are done, with `npm test` (374 files, 2431 tests), `npm run typecheck`, and `eslint` clean.

**1. Mobile rename dialog** — `src/components/home/HomeHeader.tsx:229-243`, `:511-546`
Below the `lg` breakpoint, tapping the title now opens a proper dialog with a full-size labelled input and Cancel/Save buttons, instead of the ~18rem inline field. Desktop keeps the inline edit. The split is driven by a new `useIsCompactLayout()` in `src/hooks/use-mobile.ts` (1024px, matching the `lg:` chrome the header already switches on) — I generalized the existing `ViewportManager` to take a breakpoint rather than duplicating it. New keys `session.rename.title` / `session.rename.field` are translated in all 9 locales.

**2. Folder / repo / branch in the top bar** — `HomeHeader.tsx:236-241`, `:444-453`
The repeated "omniharness" was real: the folder label and the workspace badge both fall back to the same last path segment when a run has no branch. The folder label now renders only when the badge is actually showing something different (a branch or worktree), and both are `hidden lg:*` — on phones and tablets the bar is title + ⋯ menu only. Path and branch stay reachable via the session menu, the sidebar, and the composer's workspace button on new conversations.

**3. UI font size now grows the controls** — `AppearancePreferencesManager.ts:138-153`, `globals.css`
Tailwind v4 emits every spacing utility as `calc(var(--spacing) * n)`, so the manager now writes a scaled `--spacing` onto the scale root alongside the font vars (0.92 / 0.96 / 1 / 1.15 / 1.30). This replaced ~40 lines of hand-listed CSS that only resized four square icon-button classes — padding, gaps, non-square buttons, and icons were all left behind by that approach.

I verified this in headless Chromium against the actually-compiled `globals.css`, not just by reasoning:

| | default | huge |
|---|---|---|
| icon button | 32px | 41.6px |
| icon inside it | 16px | 20.8px |
| text button height / padding | 32px / 12px | 41.6px / 15.6px |
| flex gap | 8px | 10.4px |

"default" measures byte-identical to the old baseline, so nothing shifts for anyone who hasn't touched the setting. Resizable panel widths are JS pixel values, so they stay where you dragged them — only in-component spacing scales.

**4. Composer text size** — `ConversationComposer.tsx:281`, `globals.css:435`
The textarea was hardcoded `text-[15px]`, outside every override. It now follows the **conversation** font size (one step above it, which preserves 15px at default): tiny→13px through huge→19px, with line-height following too.

Two things worth knowing:

- I tied the composer to *conversation* size rather than UI size, since what you type becomes conversation text. Say the word if you'd rather it track the UI slider.
- On iOS, an input below 16px makes Safari zoom in on focus. At "default" the composer is 15px — same as before, so no regression, but it does mean the zoom persists at default/small/tiny. I can floor it at 16px on phones if that zoom is part of what's been annoying you.

I haven't committed anything; the working tree already had unrelated changes in it when I started.

## Claude thinking · 2026-08-03T20:52:10.964Z

The commit dropdown uses responsive classes to hide on mobile and show on tablets and up, so the branch selector would disappear entirely on phones. The user is asking to remove it from mobile navigation altogether, which means they want it gone from the phone view completely.

I could add the branch to the session menu that's visible on mobile to keep it accessible, but that goes beyond what they asked for. I'll do the literal request—move it to the commit dropdown—and flag that this leaves phones without direct access to it, offering to mirror it into the mobile menu if they want it reachable there too. The mirroring pattern in the codebase is only partial—commit actions are mirrored to the ⋯ menu on mobile, but autoCommitMilestones aren't. I'm settling on adding the branch label as a DropdownMenuLabel at the top of the commit dropdown with a separator, and flagging that it won't appear in the mobile ⋯ menu, since that's where commit actions live on phones and the label would be most useful there.

For implementation, I'm removing the RunWorkspaceBadge from the header and its import, then restoring the folder label to display unconditionally (still hidden on mobile with lg:inline) since the user only asked to remove the branch, not the folder.

For the commit dropdown, I need to show the branch or worktree label there too. Rather than duplicating the parsing logic, I'll extract a shared function that resolves the workspace information—label, title, and whether it's a worktree—so both the badge and the dropdown menu can use it consistently.

Before refactoring though, I should check if RunWorkspaceBadge is still used anywhere else now that I'm removing it from the header. Let me search for other references to see if it's become dead code or if tests depend on it.

## Claude thinking · 2026-08-03T20:52:18.990Z

The `RunWorkspaceBadge` component is only used in the header right now, so if I remove it from the nav bar, it becomes unused unless the dropdown reuses it. I'm thinking the cleanest approach is to keep the component as the single source for the parse and label logic, then render the workspace badge inside the commit dropdown instead. Now I need to decide whether to render the full `RunWorkspaceBadge` component inside a `DropdownMenuLabel`, which would reuse it completely, or just show a plain text label.

Rendering the component directly in the dropdown makes sense — it keeps the visual consistency with the chip and branch icon, avoids duplicating any logic, and in a 16rem-wide dropdown the badge still reads clearly. Let me check what the test file asserts about how `RunWorkspaceBadge` is being used.

## Claude · 2026-08-03T20:54:24.609Z

Done. The branch chip is out of the nav bar entirely and now lives in the commit menus.

**Removed from the top bar** — `HomeHeader.tsx:453`
`<RunWorkspaceBadge>` is gone from the title row on all screen sizes. The folder-name label next to the title goes back to showing unconditionally on desktop (`hidden … lg:inline`) — the condition I added last turn existed only to stop it duplicating the branch chip beside it, so with the chip gone it was an orphaned quirk that would have hidden the folder except when a branch happened to be set.

**Added to the commit dropdown** — `HomeHeader.tsx:585-592`
A `DropdownMenuLabel` with the same chip sits at the top of the menu that holds "Auto-commit milestones", above a separator.

**Also mirrored into the ⋯ session menu** — `HomeHeader.tsx:494-498`
That commit dropdown is `hidden sm:flex`, so on phones it doesn't render at all — moving the branch there alone would have made it invisible on exactly the device you're using. The ⋯ menu already mirrors "Commit now" / "Commit and push now" behind `sm:hidden` for that reason, so I put the chip in the same `sm:hidden` group, right above those commit actions. Net effect: desktop sees it once (commit menu), phones see it once (⋯ menu), nobody sees it twice.

**Shared resolver** — `RunWorkspaceBadge.tsx:32-52`
The three call sites needed to agree on "is there a workspace worth showing", so the snapshot-parsing/label logic moved into an exported `resolveRunWorkspace(run, fallbackPath)` that the badge itself now consumes. This replaced the narrower `runWorkspaceBranchLabel` helper from last turn, which no longer had a caller. Both menu rows are guarded on it, so a run with no checkout path renders no empty label row.

Verification: `npm run typecheck` clean, `eslint` clean on the touched files, and the UI/app suites pass (73 files, 596 tests). I updated the two source-assertion tests in `tests/ui/conversation-actions.test.ts` that pinned the old markup — the replacement asserts the badge is *absent* from the title row and present exactly twice in the menus, so the nav bar can't silently regain it.

One caveat worth flagging: another session is actively editing this workspace — `src/server/workers/output-store.ts` and `src/server/events/named-events.ts` both changed on disk while I was running checks, and briefly broke typecheck between their two writes. Worth knowing if you see unexpected diffs; I left those files alone.

## Claude thinking · 2026-08-03T20:55:58.286Z

The issue isn't a server restart—the interface is a static build served from disk. The build in `dist/interface` was created at 19:54:53 and includes the first round of changes but not the second round from 22:53, which is why those edits aren't showing up.

The real fix is to run `pnpm build` to regenerate the static files. There's also a service worker with a `BUILD_HASH` that precaches assets, so on the phone the old UI might persist in the cache until the new service worker activates—the user may need to reload or close all tabs to see the updated version.

So the answer is: rebuild, not restart, and explain the service worker caching behavior. I should check sw.js for skipWaiting and clients.claim to tell them exactly what they'll need to do on their phone, then run the build. The build might take a minute or two, and I'll also mention that if they want a restart, `pnpm restart:control` is the proper way to do it, though it's not necessary and would kill this session.

## Claude thinking · 2026-08-03T20:56:46.097Z

So the issue is that `prepareStaticInterface` caches the index.html file at startup and validates the CSP manifest once, building security headers from the theme script hash. The handle function then serves this memoized version, which means the server continues delivering the old index.html with outdated asset filenames even after changes.

When a fresh client loads the page, it gets the old index.html pointing to asset paths that no longer exist on disk after the rebuild — those files have new hashes now. The service worker keeps existing clients working with precached assets, but new or uncached clients would hit 404s. The CSP manifest was also regenerated with a different theme script hash, but since the server's still serving the old HTML, the memoized headers remain self-consistent with it.

I was initially wrong — the server absolutely needs a restart because both index.html and CSP headers are locked in at boot time. The code confirms this. Now I need to restart the server, though there's a complication since the runner process is hosting this very agent.

There's a restart control plane already running as a separate process (PID 2879) that supervises the runner and can restart it via an HTTP endpoint. Using that path would work and automatically relaunch the server without losing the restart controller itself, though the user's UI connection will drop briefly and this agent session might terminate.

The user has now explicitly asked twice to restart the server serving the interface, so I have clear authorization. The tension is that restarting will likely kill this session mid-turn, so my final response might not reach them. I'm weighing whether to deliver the findings first and ask permission, or just proceed since they've been explicit about wanting the restart.

Looking at the process structure, the agent-runtime (PID 45806) appears to be a separate process spawned by the runner (PID 45756), both started at 2:47PM. If the runner restarts, it will likely kill and respawn agent-runtime, which means my session dies either way. The safest approach is to report my findings and trigger the restart through the restart-control endpoint, which uses supervision to minimize downtime and potentially preserve the session.

Actually, I should reconsider this. The direct-control instruction says to confirm when ambiguous, and restarting mid-turn risks dropping the session before I can deliver the diagnosis about what I found. The rebuild was necessary and is the main fix, but index.html is memoized at boot so a restart is required. I should deliver the diagnosis first, explain why the restart is needed, and ask for confirmation before proceeding — or give them the exact command to run themselves.

Since they're on a phone and have asked me to restart twice already, and the restart controller auto-relaunches the app, the downtime should be minimal. I'll use a confirmation prompt with a clear recommendation, then check the restart controller to give them accurate options.

      cwd: config.cwd,
      logFile: config.logFile,
      env: process.env,

    const entry: RestartPidEntry = {

    await system.writePidFile(entry);
    await system.appendLog(`${mode} start completed: spawned pid ${pid} (${reason})`);

  return {
    async stop(reason = "manual") {

      await system.appendLog("stop completed");
    },
    async restart(reason = "manual", mode: RestartMode = "dev") {
      await system.appendLog(`${mode} restart requested: ${reason}`);
      await stopCurrent();
      const entry = await start(mode, reason);
      await system.appendLog(`${mode} restart completed: spawned pid ${entry.pid}`);
      return entry;

    async restartCurrent(reason = "manual") {

}

function parseManagedPorts(value: string | undefined) {
  const ports = (value || "3050,5173,7800")
    .split(",")
    .map((part) => Number.parseInt(part.trim(), 10))
    .filter((port) => Number.isFinite(port) && port > 0);
  return Array.from(new Set(ports));
}

function parseCommand(value: string | undefined, fallback: { command: string; args: string[] }) {
  const parts = value?.trim().split(/\s+/g).filter(Boolean) ?? [];
  if (parts.length === 0) {
    return fallback;
  }
  return { command: parts[0] ?? fallback.command, args: parts.slice(1) };
}

export function resolveRestartControlConfig(repoRoot: string, env: Record<string, string | undefined>): RestartControlConfig {
  const commands = {
    dev: parseCommand(
      env.OMNIHARNESS_REMOTE_RESTART_DEV_COMMAND ?? env.OMNIHARNESS_REMOTE_RESTART_COMMAND,
      { command: "pnpm", args: ["run", "dev"] },
    ),
    // A restart is not first-time setup: run the lean production entry
    // (built interface + managed runner) rather than ./omniharness, which
    // is the first-run launcher (auth setup, agent ACP install, browser open,
    // tunnel guidance). Re-running that on every prod restart re-downloads
    // codex-acp and clobbers a source-built install.
    prod: parseCommand(env.OMNIHARNESS_REMOTE_RESTART_PROD_COMMAND, { command: "pnpm", args: ["run", "start"] }),
  };

  return {
    host: env.OMNIHARNESS_REMOTE_RESTART_HOST || "0.0.0.0",
    port: parsePort(env.OMNIHARNESS_REMOTE_RESTART_PORT, 3099),
    token: [REDACTED] || null,
    managedPorts: parseManagedPorts(env.OMNIHARNESS_REMOTE_RESTART_PORTS),

    cwd: repoRoot,
    commands,

## Claude · 2026-08-03T20:57:53.985Z

Here's the situation before I pull the trigger, so you have it regardless of what the restart does to this session:

**There is no Vite dev server for this project.** Your interface is a static production bundle in `dist/interface`, served by the runner (`pnpm start`, PID 45756). So there was never any HMR — every UI change needs a `vite build`.

**The build was stale.** `dist/interface` was last built at 19:54, which caught round 1 (font scaling, rename dialog) but not round 2 (branch chip moved into the commit menus). I've now rebuilt it — round 2 markers (`resolveRunWorkspace`) are in the new `HomeApp-B1PMAN3r.js`, and the new bundle is `main-CQKB0SwX.js`.

**A restart is now genuinely required, and you were right.** `src/runtime/http/static-files.ts:196` reads `index.html` **once at startup** and serves that memoized copy, along with CSP headers derived from it. The running server is still handing out the old `index.html`, which points at `main-BaBnRv0h.js` — a file my rebuild just deleted. Fresh page loads would 404 until it restarts. (Your phone is insulated for now only because the service worker precached the old assets.)

**Your phone should update on one reload.** The service worker calls `skipWaiting()` and `clients.claim()`, and its `BUILD_HASH` changed `d9c1c15e…` → `8ef7b299…`, so the new worker takes over and drops the old cache without needing a manual purge.

Restarting via the supervised controller (PID 2879, separate process) rather than killing the runner — it will relaunch `pnpm run start` automatically from the recorded `prod` mode. This will probably drop our current session; the conversation is persisted, so reopen it after the reload.

## Claude tool result: toolu_01BUvYcBoYrZ8MrMQfLfTujU · 2026-08-03T20:58:34.639Z

```text
=== scripts/dev.ts:
import { spawn, type ChildProcess } from "node:child_process";
import process from "node:process";
import { resolvePnpmArgs, resolvePnpmCommand } from "./package-manager-command";

const pnpmCommand = resolvePnpmCommand();
const pnpmArgs = (args: readonly string[]) => resolvePnpmArgs(args);
const children = new Set<ChildProcess>();
let stopping = false;

function prefixOutput(child: ChildProcess, label: string) {
  for (const stream of [child.stdout, child.stderr]) {
    stream?.on("data", (chunk) => {
      const lines = String(chunk).split(/\r?\n/);
      for (const line of lines) {
        if (line) {
          process.stdout.write(`[${label}] ${line}\n`);
        }
      }
    });
  }
}

function start(label: string, args: readonly string[]) {
  const child = spawn(pnpmCommand, pnpmArgs(args), {
    cwd: process.cwd(),
    env: process.env,
    stdio: ["inherit", "pipe", "pipe"],
  });
  children.add(child);
  prefixOutput(child, label);
  child.once("error", (error) => {
    process.stderr.write(`[${label}] ${error.message}\n`);
    void stopAll(1);
  });
  child.once("exit", (code, signal) => {
    children.delete(child);
    if (!stopping) {
      process.stderr.write(
        `[${label}] exited with ${signal ? `signal ${signal}` : `code ${code ?? "unknown"}`}\n`,
      );
      void stopAll(code && code > 0 ? code : 1);
    }
  });
  return child;
}

async function stopChild(child: ChildProcess) {
  if (child.exitCode !== null || child.signalCode !== null) {
    return;
  }
  await new Promise<void>((resolve) => {
    const timeout = setTimeout(() => {
      if (child.exitCode === null && child.signalCode === null) {
        child.kill("SIGKILL");
      }
    }, 5_000);
    child.once("exit", () => {
      clearTimeout(timeout);
      resolve();
    });
    child.kill("SIGTERM");
  });
}

async function stopAll(exitCode: number) {
  if (stopping) {
    return;
  }
  stopping = true;
  await Promise.all([...children].map(stopChild));
  process.exit(exitCode);
}

process.once("SIGINT", () => {
  void stopAll(0);
});
process.once("SIGTERM", () => {
  void stopAll(0);
});

=== vite config:
import path from "node:path";
import { fileURLToPath } from "node:url";
import react from "@vitejs/plugin-react";
import { defineConfig } from "vite";
import { interfaceCspManifestPlugin } from "./vite-csp-manifest";

const repositoryRoot = path.resolve(path.dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url)), "../..");

export default defineConfig(({ command, mode }) => {
  const packaged = mode === "packaged";
  return {
    root: path.join(repositoryRoot, "apps/interface"),
    base: packaged ? "./" : "/",
    plugins: [react(), interfaceCspManifestPlugin()],
    define: {
      __OMNI_PWA_BUILD__: JSON.stringify(command === "build" && !packaged),
    },
    resolve: {
      alias: {
        "@": path.join(repositoryRoot, "src"),
      },
    },
    build: {
      outDir: process.env.OMNIHARNESS_INTERFACE_OUT_DIR
        ? path.resolve(process.env.OMNIHARNESS_INTERFACE_OUT_DIR)
        : path.join(
          repositoryRoot,
          packaged ? "dist/interface-packaged" : "dist/interface",
        ),
      emptyOutDir: true,
      rollupOptions: {
        input: {
          index: path.join(repositoryRoot, "apps/interface/index.html"),
          "app-shell": path.join(repositoryRoot, "apps/interface/app-shell.html"),
        },
      },
    },
    server: {
      host: "0.0.0.0",
      port: Number(process.env.OMNIHARNESS_INTERFACE_PORT ?? "5173"),
      proxy: {
        "/api": {
          target: process.env.OMNIHARNESS_VITE_RUNNER_URL ?? "http://127.0.0.1:3050",
          changeOrigin: false,
        },
      },
    },
  };
});

## Claude thinking · 2026-08-03T23:36:37.069Z

So the phone UI is accessed through a Cloudflare tunnel running on https at a public hostname pointing to a local port, with Tailscale as an alternative — and this is critical for HMR because Vite's websocket needs to connect over wss:// to that same public origin.

The issue is that Vite 5+ blocks unknown hosts by default, so the tunnel hostname gets rejected. To run dev mode through the tunnel, I need to configure Vite to allow that hostname and set up HMR to use wss:// on port 443. The cleanest approach is to keep the tunnel pointing at port 3050 and have the runner proxy non-API requests to the Vite dev server on 5173, so the phone UI sees a consistent URL while HMR works seamlessly through the tunnel.

I should check whether the runner already supports this with a `--no-static` flag (which would mean they expect direct access to Vite on 5173), and verify what the cloudflared config currently maps to so I can make a concrete recommendation between changing the phone URL versus building dev-proxy support into the runner.

## Claude thinking · 2026-08-03T23:36:58.821Z

The OmniHarness public origin points to a managed tunnel running on Cloudflare's infrastructure with token-based authentication, not a local config file, so the tunnel routing is controlled remotely through the Cloudflare dashboard rather than something I can easily reconfigure locally.

Since the tunnel is locked to port 3050 and I can't redirect it to Vite's dev port 5173, the better approach is to add a dev-proxy mode to the runner itself — when a dev URL environment variable is set, the runner intercepts non-API requests and forwards them to the Vite dev server while preserving WebSocket connections for hot module reloading. The dev script would set this variable instead of using the `--no-static` flag, and I'd configure Vite to handle the tunnel hostname and use secure WebSocket connections for HMR so live updates work through the HTTPS tunnel on the phone. This keeps the public URL unchanged while enabling instant file-save feedback without rebuilds or restarts.

However, there's a service worker complication — the PWA's cached assets and app shell could interfere with HMR in dev mode, potentially requiring the user to manually unregister the SW on the phone. A simpler alternative would be to stick with production builds but add file watching to Vite so it rebuilds automatically in about 2.5 seconds, then fix the static file memoization to re-read the index and CSP headers when the build changes, eliminating the need for a restart. The user would just pull-to-refresh to see updates — not live, but fast enough and compatible with the service worker. I should present both options with a recommendation toward the dev-proxy approach since they mentioned wanting live updates.

uuid: cb4fdabc-de5e-45e9-ae8f-09cedd861a07
parent: 2e9153f2-a2e9-498c-8dd9-18311fc5f16d

## Claude · 2026-08-03T23:37:05.163Z

I've got the constraint that decides this. Your phone reaches the UI through the **managed Cloudflare tunnel** at `https://lunar-copper-harbor.omniharness.dev`, and that tunnel is token-based (run by root, configured in the Cloudflare dashboard — not in your local `config.yml`), so it points at port 3050 and I can't repoint it locally.

That rules out the obvious "just run `pnpm dev`". Dev mode runs `runner --no-static` on 3050 (API only) and puts the UI on Vite at **5173** — which your tunnel doesn't expose, so your phone would get nothing. Three things would have to change to make live reload actually work over that URL:

1. **The runner needs to proxy to Vite** (it has no proxy support today — `--no-static` assumes you hit :5173 directly). Proxying non-`/api` requests plus the WebSocket upgrade keeps your phone URL identical.
2. **Vite needs `server.allowedHosts`** for the tunnel hostname, or it rejects the request outright.
3. **`server.hmr` needs `{ protocol: "wss", clientPort: 443 }`**, since the page is https but Vite would otherwise tell the client to open `ws://…:5173`.

There's also a wrinkle either way: the installed **service worker** on your phone caches the app shell, so it can fight a dev server. Switching to dev likely needs a one-time unregister on the phone.

Two ways to go, and it's your call:
